This is a NARROW one-off prehistoric accumulation/combo exception to inherited TWANG, NOT a general-purpose infinite store, multiple-charge arsenal, cosmic invulnerability, or power to avert the extinction. How much asteroid contact/impact is truly captured, how it persisted, precise combined-release physics, Billy's days-long recovery, drunken-berries compatibility, time/place of encounter and charge aftermath remain OPEN until causally established. This must NOT be retroactively used in Episode 1 or earlier childhood, or grant a power to Eulalie. Current/past contemporary use of normal TWANG remains consistent with a historical first discovery during the earlier Godzilla fight.
NEWEST SPECIAL ROADMAP INTERPRETATION: The historical B.1–B.4 internal episode cards are FOUR ORIGINAL REVEAL ANCHORS, never the whole special length. The 22-episode roadmap is a WORKING EDITORIAL PROPOSAL ONLY; exact total, breaks and most titles NOT AUTHOR-ACCEPTED or source-committed. Audience hears Episode 1, Episode 2, Episode 3 etc., without internal B.n labels; Episode 1 title author-selected EXACT, other titles provisional. Preserve revelation order: Eulalie's lived warmth and mystery; first devour; killer identified/hat earned later; protection motive ONLY IN ACTUAL FINAL SPECIAL. Asteroid catastrophe occurs in a later movement; Godzilla first encounter much later still, NEVER in Episode 1. First Brusta fire visit pre-parasite, final second SAME-FIRE return after REAL independent exposure and elapsed time, grotesque ORGANIC only; narration/flashback duration creates zero present-day elapsed time. Main IV.4 follows finished Billy specials; 33 remaining original main overlays remain untouched.

# Billy's expanded special: causal reblock without episode quota

**Status:** Future construction overlay; **not** new numbered cards or replay authorization. Reuses the already completed `SEASON3_AND_BILLY_REWORK_REVIEW_20261010/02_BILLY_SPECIAL_REBLOCKED_OUTLINE.md` without rewriting it as settled delivery. No four-episode cap, no ten-to-twelve target, no promised fixed runtime. The four old B.1–B.4 source cards retain four **reveal anchors**, not necessarily the eventual final number. The current B.1 and B.2 were BOTH delivered provisionally; neither accepted; B.1 corrected replay remains HELD. Next new proposed public installment is Episode 3 (unwritten), not the legacy internal B.3 killer anchor. No new public card count is accepted.

## Chronology and knowledge release hard gates (R9 Pro QAPX candidate)

1. Young Billy and Mama Eulalie roughly 110 million years ago; mother's killer and final protection motive remain protected reveals, not literal birth date certification.
2. After early childhood at about 66 million years ago: the actual extinction impact, unsuccessful block, intact banjo, and DAYS-LATER waking. No Billy banjo note after waking.
3. Millions of years after asteroid: FIRST genuine Godzilla encounter, atomic-beam interception, mocking-song first note, ACCIDENTAL unique combined TWANG emitted toward Godzilla. Exact Godzilla era, incarnation, hit, winner and relative placement to the Ice Age remain OPEN.
4. Ice Age occurs LATER THAN extinction in real chronology. Sid/Zion never devoured; Manny/Diego/Scrat selected for future devouring with fate unresolved. The optional Godzilla beat must be placed before/after this era only once its actual date is causally fixed.
5. Much later physical early-2000s Chris/Billy fusion, then the present D215/R67 campfire with no added day from narration. Only lawful spoken relay gives Billy access to Chris's private dream. Second organic Brusta revisit requires REAL elapsed present time and established infection, currently HOLD.

## Reveal order, responsibility, and proof

| Anchor | Objective revelation | Focalizer / knowledge | Retained dramatic purpose | Quality and cause test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Opening / B.1: ALOYSIUS | Mama Yoo-LAY-lee's actual warmth; Aloysius childlike fear; red/green clues after her disappearance | Young Billy is asleep during 12-hour fight; adult storyteller cannot claim he witnessed it | Audience understands safety and loss without knowing killer/protective motive | Tom Kench speaks to infant before valid departure; no cigarettes; Grandfather William; exact complete author-approved Candidate C MORE'N YOU KNOW only when whole sung performance is earned. Billy is called Billy from infancy, but the audience explanation is delayed; maternal banjo-origin disclosure is separate. B.1 held. |
| B.2: THE FIRST DEVOUR | Young prey-minded Billy's first physical real devour and altered self-image | Child's physical limits, surprise, predator resisting/acting | An irreversible survival choice, not a montage of power upgrades | First swallow actually occurs under danger; source system and agency tested; no adult fully equipped apex baseline at start. |
| Middle / legacy B.3: THE BIG ASS NIGGA SAURUS | Specific mother's killer revealed and physically confronted; hat hide comes from him | Must learn identity through legitimate evidence, not invented omniscience | Revenge grants a result but not the protective explanation | Twelve-hour battle had multiple adversaries; mostly nonverbal dinosaurs; no second Giganotosaurus hat; target fights credibly. |
| Final / legacy B.4: THE SKY DON'T GIVE A FUCK | Mama's protective motive understood; Brusta's SECOND same-fire arrival and Billy's successful hunting/swallowing | Billy's first person observations with physically permitted present-day sounds | Family tenderness transforms into real predator response without a metaphor lecture | Organic exposed Brusta; no cybernetics; exact three-monkey author lines; intact conjoined body plus separate organism to CATFISH PENITENTIARY; Vecna not omniscient. |

## Segment into episodes ONLY after these earned-break tests

A movement qualifies for its own new special **only if** (a) it has a distinct external or interpersonal opposition, (b) Billy's choices change the situation, (c) the resolution creates a consequence that the next movement inherits, (d) its arc differs materially from adjacent movements, and (e) it has enough original performance material for a non-padded TTS listen. Otherwise combine it with the nearest causal sibling. Long worthwhile episodes remain legal. Record a new numbered card only during a later authorized rewrite/approval, never by a research document.

### Proposed causal movements (not guaranteed episodes)

**M1 The safety of Mama:** Mama/William/Tom and young Aloysius's genuine humor, safety, and mother's ability to read his bluffs. No adult predator speech transplanted onto baby. **Change:** discovery of loss with ambiguous red/green evidence. **Note:** preserve B.1 provisional and replay HOLD.

**M2 Prey becomes agent:** Near-misses, nonverbal dinosaur encounters and independent prehistoric friends, one situation where polite advice fails. **Change:** first devour, a real act with contested agency; immediate aftermath shows change in practical behavior. **Legacy:** Original B.2 FIRST DEVOUR anchor is already delivered provisionally, NOT accepted; do not replay by construction work.

**M3 The domestic nightmare, strictly secondary:** Chris's apparent PSN and RDR2 destruction, waking overlap BONELESS LASAGNA, delayed revelation on actual wake before attempted Kancho impact. This belonged to the delivered-provisional Episode 2 plan, **not** a new mandatory special or permission to replay it. No early word 'dream' or subtitle spoiler. **Narrator-safe conditional:** Billy may report only Chris's own later spoken description, without framing the coming account as a dream; document that disclosure before narration and restrict interior specifics to what Chris conveys. If an effective no-spoiler framing cannot satisfy the first-person rule, this component is held for source-safe redesign rather than invoking Mimir/telepathy. Dream Chris's experience doesn't delete real saves; historical Venlee AC IV incident alone is real.

**M4 Growing kin and trust:** Gnar genuinely Billy's maternal uncle/friend, prehistoric companions with distinct communication, nonmandatory local rivalries. **Change:** somebody Billy values refuses him or he must adjust a habit. Do not translate Gnar or turn ordinary dinosaurs into English speakers.

**M5 Vengeance:** Follow actual clues to the killer, confront with genuine resistant prey/predator behavior and material damage, acquire killer's hide. **Change:** Billy avenges Mama yet fails to understand WHY she protected him. No guaranteed psychological closure.

**M6 Asteroid first; immense later history:** AFTER childhood and the pre-extinction movements, the REAL ~66-million-year asteroid strikes. Billy attempts to block it with Eulalie's single banjo; the extinction still happens. He awakes days later to a changed world and learns that the banjo is intact and indestructible. CONTINUITY LOCK FOR A LATER UNSCHEDULED SCENE, NOT AN M6 PERFORMANCE: Billy plays NO note after awakening until an authentic first Godzilla encounter MILLIONS OF YEARS later. In THAT later encounter, he deliberately intercepts Godzilla's source-valid atomic BEAM with the same banjo, starts a mocking song, and his FIRST plucked note ACCIDENTALLY releases the one-off combined asteroid/atomic TWANG TOWARD Godzilla. The incarnation, hit, winner, precise era and order relative to the Ice Age are UNKNOWN. Do not perform this Godzilla scene in M6 merely because this rule is documented here; locate it only after its source-valid date is determined. Other substantially causal centuries (including selected Hawking and Jesus encounters) retain independent era/access and Jesus fate UNKNOWN. No source-causal guest travel invented.

**M7 Ice Age / paradox (AFTER 66-Ma extinction; Godzilla relative placement OPEN):** Distinct ties to Manny, Diego, Scrat and selected devourings with digestion/death still unresolved; Sid/Zion fused SOUL and NEVER devoured; Zion's exact 'oh i forgot' only upon legitimately triggered meeting. **Change:** Billy's trust, attachment, or long-memory confidence is tested, not automatic cosmic explanation.

**M8 Late prehistoric-to-modern bridge (NOT a second extinction):** The asteroid and its ecological aftermath belong at M6, long before the Ice Age. Do not move the 66-Ma catastrophe after Ice Age friendships or repeat it here. Handle the eventual legitimately dated Godzilla encounter at its open chronological slot after the asteroid, before the contemporary era, without asserting whether it preceded or followed the Ice Age. The long later passage culminates in the **physical** early-2000s Mardi Gras Chris/Billy fusion, never astral. The original spoon remains the material bridge. **Change:** Billy has a new family without surrendering sovereignty.

**M9 Two interleaved present-campfire visits:** First visit pre-parasite with exact established voluntary exit/one-bite warning; same campfire later second post-exposure organic body-horror return. The final interruption leads to the authorized real bayou hunt and conjoined swallow (one intake, two minds, third organism). Billy's triple-monkey exact speech is not narrated as a decoded metaphor; leave listener to infer. **Change:** Brusta and parasite arrive in penitentiary custody; DO NOT show prebuilt Charles warden suite or instant Vecna takeover.

| First campfire Brusta warning | Future-selected for special telling | Audience can know Brian earned a serious warning | Vecna NOT present/exposed, cannot possess that observation retroactively |

| Brusta→prison | Selected actual final special outcome | Audience may get brief post-swallow prison shock, not entire subplot | Facility/cell routing must be actually Billy-chosen; Vecna targets only eligible inmates |

## Delivery and memory constraints

The campfire isn't a magical paused-time simulation. WARNING: the selected second Brusta same-fire return CANNOT be played at frozen D215 unless independent PRESENT-DAY elapsed time and credible exposure have actually occurred; narrating 100 million years does not advance that clock. Hold its placement rather than silently slipping extra days into a flashback. The Sins at the fire have distinct physical locations; Chris communicates outward via banjo only when permitted, not telepathically; Charles remains inside. Billy controls exterior and route, retains the full narrator role. RDR2 references must be audible and supported; no fake sound-effect text and no full lullaby reprise for a mere memory. The historic 110-million-year recollection is narrated time, never a new main-calendar day.

## Open, deferred, and truly decided

**Decided / selected future:** Mother/killer/motive order, first devour delivery-provisional status, first/final Brusta visit selection, organic-only future return, selected future successful prison swallow, special narrator, delivered-provisional dream fakeout and lasagna echo, no padded length. None of these future choices means enacted present-day exposure or swallow. **Deferred to actual causality/author authority:** episode count and card IDs, Brusta exposure/rate, Godzilla identity/winner, Sid/Zion mechanism, exact Meatwad or prison room pathways, Jesus fate. **Never inferred:** future delivery, new historical deaths, romance, or automatic prisoner escape.

## Release candidate interpretation
This is a prospective construction guide, subordinate to newest corrections and full Billy owners. The four B.1–B.4 identifiers are historical REVEAL ANCHORS, not consecutive public episodes; total open. No padded runtime. The idea banks are development, not guaranteed events. B.1 corrected replay HELD; B.2 delivered provisional NOT ACCEPTED; public Episode 3 remains unwritten. Main IV.4 only after genuinely completed specials.

<!-- BEGIN LOGICAL OWNER: KAZE_Act_XIV_Episode_2.txt | ROLE: historical_evidence -->
They had barely gone two streets from the ferry house when Billy remembered the man in his belly.

“Chris. Our guest has begun discussing his rights.”

Chris stopped beside a drain. Frank turned back. Behind them, the officers were still taking possession of Hollis and the wreckage of his retrieval party.

“The guy with the pistol?” Chris asked.

“The very same. Tried to put a hole in his host, then complained about the accommodations. I have entertained better-mannered parasites.”

Frank pointed back toward the officers. “Let him out.”

“I was fixing to. But now it sounds like your idea.”

Arthur took the cigar from his teeth. “Reckon history’ll survive the confusion.”

Billy grumbled. Chris’s jaw opened down past his chest, and those great hands reached out through the living seam. Billy pulled himself into the street as Chris’s body rolled inward around him. The inversion finished with Billy outside and Chris back among his screens and clutter. Christ, the things a man will tolerate to remain near his cookware.

Billy opened his jaws again, this time keeping the top of his broad head and weathered hat steady. His tongue delivered the swallowed gunman onto the cobbles in a wet, furious heap. The man’s pistol remained inside.

“There,” Billy said. “Alive, ungrateful, and no longer my responsibility.”

Frank called the officers over. The man tried to stand, saw Frank, and reconsidered.

Billy watched them take him away.

“Had a little bastard act just like that before the sky fell. Always wanted the best crossing. Never once thanked the fellow who ate the competition.”

“You helped him?” Arthur asked.

“I improved his circumstances.”

Arthur put his cigar back. “That ain’t what I asked.”

Billy touched his hat brim and declined to answer. I’ll give Arthur this: he could leave a question sitting there like a loaded gun. Never felt obliged to fire it for you.

Seven days later, Billy was still complaining about a different audience.

“An entire age, gentlemen. Gone before it could properly appreciate me.”

He was walking toward the commune with his banjo hanging against his belly. Daniel rode an easy, drunken balance along a low wall. Kaze had fallen half a pace behind Frank, watching him roll his repaired shoulder beneath his coat.

“The asteroid,” Arthur said.

“The interruption. There is a distinction. I was entering my prime.”

Chris’s voice came through the banjo. “You’d already been alive forty-something million years.”

“A development period.”

“Man, that’s a long time to be somebody’s promising nephew.”

Daniel lost his balance laughing, caught the wall with his tail, and swung himself back upright without spilling his drink.

“Exactly,” Billy said, pointing at him. “That sort of recovery. I had footwork people crossed rivers to witness.”

“Or they were getting away,” Arthur said.

“Same distance. Entirely different attitude.”

Kaze grinned at that. Billy noticed, warmed to his subject, and began describing a riverbank using three different present-day buildings as markers. When Arthur questioned the distance, Billy became offended by the modern buildings.

“You put everything so close together now. Of course it looks unimpressive.”


The dog’s nose lifted. His attention moved from the road to the narrow service approach beside the commune. There were boots there, wagon wheels, familiar oil, and the metallic scrape of something being unloaded.


Two Noxian transport wagons stood beyond the turn. One carried folded restraint frames. The other carried a low wheeled treatment bed and crates with the same heavy fittings the gang had seen .

Kaze’s smile stayed for another moment after the amusement had gone.

“Oh. They found more.”

At the commune doorway, Vi had both hands planted on the first wagon’s shaft.

“Move it back.”

A soldier answered that the bed was for transport.

“Then transport it somewhere else.”

Jinx sat above them on the doorway lintel, one boot swinging. Her pistol rested across her thigh. Behind the open door, Warwick’s great shape filled the adjoining room where he had chosen to remain with his daughters.

The soldiers had brought a bed for him. They had not persuaded him to get on it. A remarkable amount of military planning had gone into avoiding that small difficulty.

“You can keep calling it medical,” Jinx said. “Those little straps still do the same thing.”

Viktor stood between the room and the courtyard. He had asked the sisters to consider moving Warwick to the better-equipped annex. Vi had heard enough of that invitation once the wagons arrived with armed escorts.

Now she saw Kaze looking at the folded frames.

“They haven’t taken him,” she said immediately.

“I see him.” Kaze leaned sideways to catch Warwick’s eyes through the door. “What’s up, big man?”

Warwick’s lip lifted. His gaze dropped , then returned to the soldiers.

“Lovely reunion,” Jinx said. “Can somebody tell the delivery boys we’re not signing?”

Ekko came out through the side passage carrying a tool roll. Heimerdinger followed, and Jayce ducked beneath the lintel behind them, Mercury Hammer held low.

Vi had asked Ekko to come because her father’s brief relief under Viktor’s hand had left her with a question she couldn’t punch into answering. Ekko had brought Heimerdinger. Jayce had come to look at what his renewed Hexcore cooperation had helped make possible.

They had arrived to examine a treatment. Now they were standing beside a military delivery. The straps were neatly rolled. The rifle barrels were clean. Some conscientious bastard had prepared the whole bloody parcel.

“These weren’t here when we came in,” Ekko told Vi.

“They waited until you were busy,” she said.

Kaze walked between the wagons. A soldier moved into his path, then recognized him. The man’s spear stayed up, but his feet shifted away from the wheel.

“You know what happened at your depot?” Kaze asked.

The soldier said nothing.

“Look at me, hermano. You brought the same shit down a different road. What did you think I was mad about? The road?”

Frank’s eyes moved over the crates, the escort, the doorway. His repaired shoulder worked cleanly as he settled the sling of his rifle. A replacement plate sat beneath the coat where the depot fight had damaged the old one. He had serviced it. It was back in use.

“Who ordered the move?” he asked.

“General Medarda,” the soldier answered.

Vi pushed the wagon backward another inch.

“He said no.”

Viktor looked toward the doorway. “The equipment is intended to reduce the risk of—”

“No,” Warwick said.

Aye. A small word from such a large mouth. Everyone heard it perfectly.

Billy rested one hand on the wagon’s folded frame.

“Young men used to come into my water with contraptions, too. Different contraptions. Same confidence. You could always tell which one had explained the plan to his friends.”

The soldier glanced at the banjo.

Billy smiled.

“Don’t look there, darling. That ain’t the part you’ll have trouble fitting through.”

Behind Viktor, a man sat on the edge of a treatment couch. Huck’s skin bore the pale, branching traces of his healing. His hands were steady until the raised voices drew his attention toward the door.

One wrist rested in a small brass cradle connected to the treatment stand by a flexible lead. Viktor had fitted it that morning while Jayce watched: a first attempt to sustain this particular treatment through an external contact instead of keeping his hand on the patient.

Huck had agreed to try it. He had not agreed to a wagon.

“I’m staying,” he said.

He started to lift his wrist. Viktor gently asked him to wait until they had assessed the connection.

Kaze heard both sentences.

His attention moved from the wagons to Huck’s hand.

“Why does he need that?”

“His body has improved,” Viktor said. “Maintaining independent coordination has been more difficult.”

“More difficult for who?”

“For him.”

“Then ask him.”

Viktor turned toward Huck, but a shout outside cut through the exchange. One of the escort had gone around the wagon. Vi saw him reaching past her for the room door and knocked his arm aside.

His companion lowered a rifle.

Frank moved first, shoving the barrel up. The shot tore the doorframe above Vi’s head. Warwick came through the doorway with a roar that drove Huck back against the couch.

The next soldier fired at Warwick. Oh, that was clever. Wake the violence in a creature, then stand close enough to inspect your work.

The bullet cut the beast’s shoulder. Warwick struck him across the chest and threw him into the wagon wheel. Wood broke.

The dog pulled. The weapon jerked out of the soldier’s hands.

Vi caught Warwick’s gaze.

“Dad. Here. Look at me.”

Jinx dropped from the lintel beside her. “We’re right here.”

Blood ran down Warwick’s arm. His eyes caught on it, then on the man beneath the wagon. Vi kept herself in front of that line of sight while Jinx moved along his other side.

Kaze turned toward the rifleman behind Frank.

A dark opening split the space in front of the raised muzzle. The shot vanished through it and burst into the empty cobbles behind the wagons. Kaze stepped close enough to touch the man’s sleeve.

“You see him?” He pointed at Frank. “That one. You keep making me stop what I’m doing.”

The rifleman swung the stock. Kaze slipped inside it, drew the Black Marrow, and drove the blade beneath his jaw. The point came out above the collar at the back. Kaze pulled it free and let him fall.

The escort broke into movement. Some sought cover. Others tried to pull the first wagon away.

Arthur shot the man reaching for a mounted gun before he finished climbing onto its step. Daniel sprang from the low wall onto the second wagon, his spear held in one bare foot and his bottle secured by his tail.

“You boys delivering beds or collecting customers?” he asked.

A sword came up at him. Daniel leaned too far backward to look recoverable, caught the wagon rail with one hand, and let the cut pass over his stomach. His foot drove the spearhead through the attacker’s knee.

“Bad service either way.”

He pulled the spear free and landed sideways on the rail, grinning through the man’s scream. A second soldier came at his back. Daniel’s pale eyes caught the movement; the chain blade passed from his hand to his foot, curled around the attacker’s sword wrist, and pulled him into a heel strike that broke his mouth against the wagon edge.

Then the first wagon lurched.

A horse dragged the broken wheel. The axle caught the treatment lead running beside the doorway, and the brass cradle jerked against Huck’s wrist.

Ekko saw Huck’s hand open.

The man fell forward with his eyes wide and his legs making no useful attempt to catch him. Ekko reached him, felt him breathing, and heard a thin sound that never became a word.

His hand closed on the Z-Drive.

Four seconds folded back.

The wagon stood where it had been. The horse pulled again. Huck was upright on the couch, and nobody but Ekko remembered his fall.

“Axle! Catch the axle!” Ekko shouted.

He caught the wagon shaft, planted his feet, and stopped it before the wheel reached the lead.

“I have it.”

Ekko pulled the cable clear and moved the treatment stand inside.

Kaze looked from him to Huck.

“What happened?”

“He fell. Couldn’t move. Still breathing.”

Kaze glanced at the device on Ekko’s back, then at the unbroken connection.

“That little turn-back thing?”

“Keep this clear,” Ekko said. “We need to find out what it’s doing to him.”

Kaze wiped the Black Marrow on the dead rifleman’s coat.

“Fine. That one stays.”

He pointed the bloody blade at the transport frames.

“These don’t.”

He lowered himself as he grew, keeping the wagon under control rather than lifting it over the doorway.

Raised hackles shone within a gold aura, his white markings still clear. The dog’s larger paws dug into the road, and his braced body filled the gap the soldiers had been using to approach Warwick.


Take people back.”

Vi and Jinx eased Warwick away from the door. He resisted at first, staring at the blood on his arm. Jinx put her hand where he could see it.

“You already broke their wheel. Come admire it from inside.”

His breathing roughened, then slowed enough for him to follow.

On the courtyard’s far side, soldiers were hauling another folded frame toward the service passage. Billy watched their hands on its handles.

“Now that,” he said, “is an ambitious little piece of furniture.”

His thumb struck a dead string. The next notes rolled away beneath his voice, quick and bright, while water darkened the dust around his feet.

“Made the bed first.
That’s hospitality, I suppose.
Measured a fellow while he was still standing—
you boys buy boots the same way?
Just tell the feet what size they ought to be?”

The men pulled harder. Mud reached the wagon rut. Billy followed them, banjo high against his chest, one thumb keeping the pulse while his fingers left a little space for the words to stumble into.

“Ain’t a guest room if the door only opens from your side.
But you put a white sheet on it.
Well.
My goodness.
That ought to wash your hands.”

One soldier fired. The shot struck Billy’s shoulder and drew a wet furrow. He stopped picking long enough to look down at the wound.

“Darling, I was speaking.”

He resumed with a hard slap against the banjo head. The water had reached the soldier’s ankles. Behind Billy, Arthur kept the opposite firing lane clear with two quick shots, while Frank moved the wounded man beside the broken wheel away from its settling weight.

“They used to send the young ones down to my bank.
Said, go ask him.
He knows the crossing.
And I did.
Knew who’d make it over, too.
Terrible thing, a reputation.
Folks come to you for directions
then get personal about the destination.”

The frame carriers abandoned one handle. Billy’s tongue caught the other man around the waist and dragged him off the service step. He let the banjo swing on its strap while his hand took the man’s rifle. The weapon broke across Billy’s knee.

“I was supposed to be somebody,” he told the struggling soldier. “Had the whole bank watching. Then the sky arrived without an invitation.”

Chris’s voice came through the banjo. “You were somebody. You’re describing being a local hazard.”

Billy pulled the man close enough to inspect his expression.

“Hear that? This is the support I receive at home.”

He shoved the soldier down into the rising bayou, held him under for a moment, then lifted him coughing onto the step. The man crawled away from the water. Billy let him. He had become more interested in the soldiers still trying to save the equipment.

The rolling notes returned, then stopped abruptly beneath an ordinary spoken sentence.

“Chris seasons a pan till it can give testimony.
Calls it building character.
I seen things grow on his equipment
that ought to be paying rent.”

“That’s polymerized oil, you prehistoric bitch.”

“Mm-hm. Nice to know it has a profession.”

Inside, Chris had the live exterior view on his second monitor, sound arriving with it. He saw the rear soldier lift a crossbow above the frame.

“Left of the doorway. Bow coming up.”

Billy turned. The bolt struck his raised banjo. His answering pluck sent it back through the crossbowman’s chest, carrying him against the wall.

The note kept ringing after the body stopped. Aye, there was his appreciative audience at last. Nailed to the fucking wall.

Billy looked toward the roof as though there were still an audience to disappoint.

“Used to could do that across a river,” he said. “River’s gone now. Everybody acts like that settles the argument.”

Arthur shot a soldier who tried to escape with the frame’s locking assembly. The brass bundle fell into the mud beside him.

Billy bent to pick it up. Strings ticked beneath his thumb again.

“You call it holding steady.
I call it keeping hold.
Funny how the same hand
can help you up
or keep you there.

Depends where it lets go.

There’s your little buckle.
Pretty teeth on it.
You were going to close that over somebody’s wrist
and call the fit a kindness.”

He put the assembly between his own jaws. Metal cracked. He spat the pieces into the wagon bed.

“I lost an audience to the sky.
Never did forgive the timing.
But you boys—
you keep yours strapped down.
Must be nice, knowing everybody’ll stay for the finish.”

The last frame carrier drew a knife and lunged while Billy’s hands were on the instrument. The knife fell. Billy’s next breath became a chuckle.

“Now there’s a fellow with proper follow-through.”

Billy looked offended when the dog failed to stay for the compliment.

“Lord. Everybody’s busy.”

He took the folded frame by its hinges and opened it the wrong way. The metal shrieked, and its restraints came apart under his hands.


Daniel landed beside him, cheeks flushed, laughing too hard at something nobody had heard.

“The straps?”

“The people.”

Daniel’s smile thinned. He looked at the dead rifleman near the door, then at Huck sitting inside.

“Fair.”

He put his drink down, drew a hair from his head, and worked it into a small pick. His hands shook with drunken excess; the tool did not.

By then the bayou had spread through the far courtyard and beneath the service passage. Billy left the occupied room dry. The surviving soldiers had gone through its outer edge, dragging those wounded who could still be moved. Frank let them withdraw.

Arthur watched until they cleared the lane.

“Expensive delivery,” he said.

He searched the wagon’s iron-bound cash box, found it locked, and held it out to Daniel.

“Put them fingers to work again.”

Daniel picked up his drink first. “You say that like I owe you.”

“You owe me a better joke than the one you’re thinking of.”

Daniel considered him. Then he laughed, opened the box, and took the first handful of silver before Arthur could reach in.

“There. Now you owe me.”

I suppose that’s one way to establish a banking system.

Inside, Heimerdinger was looking at Huck’s hand.

The fighting had stopped. Huck kept turning his wrist inside the brass cradle, testing the little freedom it allowed him.

“I want to see,” he said. “What happens without it.”

Viktor knelt beside him. “There is a risk.”

“He knows,” Ekko said. “We’re telling him.”

He explained the fall he alone remembered. Huck listened without looking away from his wrist. Jayce moved the couch so the man could lie back before they touched the connection.

Chris watched from his monitor while Billy stood in the doorway. His voice came softly through the banjo.

“Man. That thing looks like a wedding ring somebody put on your steering wheel.”

“Can you see the contact from there?” Jayce asked.

“Upper latch. His sleeve’s over the bottom. Don’t pull against the arm.”

Jayce adjusted it. Chris went quiet.

Heimerdinger took Huck’s free hand. Ekko stood beside the contact, ready to restore it. Viktor stayed close enough to intervene. Vi watched from Warwick’s doorway, her father settled behind her with Jinx.

Huck lay back.

“Open it.”

Ekko lifted the contact.

Huck’s fingers stopped responding. His legs stayed where they had been placed. His eyes moved toward Heimerdinger, and his mouth worked once without completing the word.

Heimerdinger kept hold of his hand. “Restore it.”

Ekko lowered the contact immediately.

Huck drew a sharp breath. His fingers closed around Heimerdinger’s. The wee fellow let him grip as hard as he needed. I doubt either of them was thinking about the instrument readings then.

The pale healed skin had held its shape throughout. His breathing and pulse had continued. Whatever had changed his body had not simply fallen away when his own control did.

“I was trying,” Huck said.

“I saw,” Heimerdinger answered.

Jayce stared at the returning light in the treatment stand.

“That response,” he said to Viktor. “The correction keeps passing through the connection.”

Viktor looked at him. “It sustains what the body cannot yet sustain alone.”

“The Hexcore did that in our tests. Changed its response when the living tissue changed. We let it keep correcting.”

Ekko took out his notebook. He wrote the position of the contact and what Huck had actually managed before and after it opened. Heimerdinger added the continuing pulse and breathing, then the movement that returned.

“We have work to do,” Heimerdinger said.

Vi stepped closer. “Does that happen to him?”

She meant her father.

Heimerdinger looked back at Warwick’s different body, the wounded shoulder already drawing closed.

“I cannot tell you that from Huck.”

Jinx had been crouched beside Warwick, watching the wound close. Now she looked toward the cradle.

“So nobody tests it by yanking Dad out of something.”

“Agreed,” Ekko said.

Kaze entered with the Black Marrow sheathed. He leaned over Huck, then crouched until their eyes were level.

“You still want to stay?”

“I want to be able to leave.”

Kaze nodded once. His gaze moved to Viktor.

“Hear how those ain’t the same answer?”

“He needs support.”

“He needs support. Okay. And you brought a fucking wagon. You heard ‘help me stand’ and started picking where he’d stand.”

“I did not order the escort.”

Kaze looked toward the destroyed bed. He seemed ready to say something, then caught himself and laughed without much pleasure.

“You didn’t order those either, did you? The soldiers. That’s the part you keep clean?”

Viktor’s face tightened.

“The attempt was mine as well.”

“There you go. Fuck. There you go.” Kaze stood, palms spread. “That’s a whole sentence with you in it.”

Frank came into the doorway. “He stays connected. They figure out how he can leave.”

Kaze looked at him, then at the surviving treatment stand.

“I already said that one stays.”

“Then keep it that way.”

For a moment, Kaze’s mouth moved around an answer he didn’t give. He turned back to Viktor instead.

“I want you to see what we took apart. Come outside.”

Viktor glanced at Huck. Heimerdinger settled beside the couch, and Ekko remained at the contact. Jayce picked up his hammer and followed Viktor into the courtyard.

The transport beds lay open, rails torn away. The calibrated restraint fittings were broken in the mud. Arthur and Daniel were counting the escort’s silver on a crate lid.

“No room for a decent shoulder turn,” Billy said. “Whoever built this never had to contend with an athlete.”

“You?” Chris asked.

“Among others.”

“Your famous knee?”

“Wasn’t the knee that failed me. It was the opportunity.”


“Now frame fail.”

Billy watched the halves come together, then nodded approvingly. “Yes. That is considerably closer to the spirit of my point.”

Kaze stood beside the wreckage and waited for Viktor to look at it.

“You’re going to keep helping people?”

“Yes.”

“Then figure out how they get to tell you they’re done.”

Jayce stayed beside his former partner. “I’ll come back for Huck. I want the treatment notes you made before this connection.”

Viktor studied him.

“You believe I have harmed him.”

“I helped you make this possible,” Jayce said. “I’m going to find out what that means.”

Viktor’s gaze dropped to a smear of Warwick’s blood on the threshold.

After the others turned away, he took a collection vial from the treatment tray. He gathered a little of the fresh material with a clean instrument and sealed it.

Jinx saw him stand.

“What’s that?”

“A sample. His recovery may help us understand—”

“He said no.”

Viktor looked at the vial, then at her.

“He refused transport.”

She held out her hand.

He did not give it to her.

Jayce turned back at her silence. Viktor put the vial on the treatment tray in front of them.

“There is regenerative activity we have not accounted for. Singed may be able to explain it.”

“You want to keep using him,” Vi said from the door.

“I want to help the people whose bodies cannot survive further change.”

“Then ask him for it.”

Warwick looked from the little glass vessel to Viktor. The room filled with the low machinery of his growl.

Viktor left the vial on the tray. Even I could hear what that growl meant. The answer had reached him twice now, and there he stood, still considering the glass.

Jayce took it before he could return for it.

“Caitlyn gets this,” he said. “And an account of where it came from.”

Jinx kept looking at Viktor even after Jayce stepped away. She had seen people take pieces of a person and call the pieces useful before.

Billy let the bayou recede. Mud became the courtyard’s broken paving again, and the smashed fittings rose onto it. He gathered the loose buckles into one wagon bed, inspected the remaining hinges, and selected two for Chris.

“Those have a decent temper,” he said.

Chris’s voice answered from inside. “Bring the axle pins, too.”

Kaze looked at the growing salvage pile.

“Nigga, we just broke this shit.”

“Yeah. Makes the good parts easier to get.”

His body had begun settling back toward its ordinary size. Once he had returned fully to baseline, he added, “You’ll complain when we need one later.”

Kaze stared at him, then laughed. “Both of you. Every fucking time.”

The dog went after it, delighted by the part of the day that finally made sense.

Ekko came to the doorway before the gang left.

“Heimer’s staying with Huck. I’m coming back with what we need to compare the treatment. Leave that stand alone.”

Arthur closed the cash box with his heel. “We got everything we came to bust.”

“And everything we came to steal,” Daniel said.

“You came with a list?”

Daniel patted the money beneath his sash. “Flexible list.”

Frank watched them lift the box into the usable wagon bed. He stayed long enough to see that the soldiers had withdrawn from the approach and that Vi could close her father’s door herself.

Nobody asked her to sign anything.

That evening, Caitlyn placed the sealed vial between herself and Singed, with Jayce’s account beside it. The doctor remained behind the detention-room grating. She opened the vessel herself and allowed him to take a small portion onto a glass slide under her eye.

“Vander did not agree to further collection,” she said.

Singed examined the material, then looked at the closing wound described in Jayce’s account.

“His regenerative properties were the purpose of the work.”

“I asked what Viktor was collecting it for.”

“Bodies undergoing profound change require stabilization. This may offer a way to keep them viable. A sample is only a beginning.”

“And Viktor would need you.”

“He would need someone who understands what was done to Vander.”

Caitlyn capped the vial and put it back in her case.

“You remain here.”

Singed watched the case close. “Then you will have to decide which questions you wish answered here.”

Across the city, Warwick sat with his daughters in the room he had chosen. Vi had shut the door against the courtyard. Jinx had placed the little brass latch she’d taken from the transport bed on the floor between her boots.

She turned it over once, then threw it through the open window.

Warwick watched it go.

“Powder.”

She looked up.

His great hand opened beside her.

For once, she left the broken thing where it landed.

NARRATION, EPISODE PRESENTATION AND AUDIO READABILITY

Owns the practical presentation route. canon/presentation/PRESENTATION.md remains the complete source for unchanged narration and delivery behavior. Current_Continuity_Source.txt owns the starting point; the active Season 3 outline owns the fifteen future working cards; the completed S2 plan is historical. This file does not create a second plot.

BLACK MIMIR
Every ordinary non-dialogue PLAY sentence is Black Mimir speaking as himself. Preserve his lived GOW history, Scottish/Celtic rhythm, profanity, curiosity, diplomatic intelligence, uncertainty, humor and genuine emotional seriousness. He may be shaken or go quiet. Do not replace him with a neutral announcer, phonetic accent caricature or an omniscient intelligence service. The narrow already approved Season 3 Deadpool narration hijack stays bounded; it does not establish routine narrator rotation.

Mimir recognizes Thor and the World Serpent through remembered source experience. At their actual finale appearance he can react, name what he recognizes for the listener and separate it from what he cannot know. Seeing Thor alive contradicts parts of Mimir's remembered history; recognition is not proof of the exact source instant. His meaningful first reaction happens once in XVI.1, not again as a fresh discovery in S3. He remains narration-only unless an independently established physical rule says otherwise. Any interpretation of the serpent's utterance presented to the audience does not become information in nearby human ears.

SCENES AND VOICES
Orient a new location or focal thread through place, immediate desire, obstacle and consequence. Preserve whose eyes/ears can perceive what, especially inside BELLY versus the exterior. Give a new consequential figure enough appearance, equipment, behavior and relationship context to be understood without a game recap. Choose connected action and speech; isolated fragments are emphasis, not a default paragraph generator.

Read each active speaker's own character source and the relevant pairwise/group source. An influence never replaces the person. Chris's humor can meet agreement, indifference, contradiction or real hurt without the same recovery loop. Billy may sustain a chosen original performance and vary structure, metaphor, meaning and rhyme; no obligatory couplet format or recycled sourced lyrics.

Plot motion and quieter behavior can coexist. Ordinary games, repairs and arguments matter when somebody makes a choice and another person responds. Urgent threat is acted on promptly. A scene need not include the entire gang or repeat a running gag simply to prove it was remembered. Preserve consequences between cuts and between episodes, including blood, injuries, broken property, withdrawn trust and public accounts.

FOURTH WALL AND PRODUCTION FIREWALL
It does not grant Arthur or Daniel new audience awareness. It does not permit production notes, prompts, research references, package corrections, episode-plan explanations or internal validation commentary inside PLAY. Use Sins_Group_and_Reputation_Source.txt for the permission boundaries.

READ-ALOUD DELIVERY
For a resend, begin with the actual episode beginning and provide the complete requested text; a repost is not a new delivery. For new PLAY, use the current unique cursor. This source update itself generates no episode or audio and cannot certify that a playback button starts correctly.

Use clear attribution and natural punctuation, particularly in rapid exchanges. Describe the World Serpent's low, layered resonance and physical effect in readable prose; do not use pages of phonetic rumbling or fake reconstructed ancient language. Sound-design research is a writing reference, not a claim that a generic TTS engine reproduces the game's voice. Existing runtime targets remain planning guidance; no runtime measurement is claimed without rendered audio.

CHECK ONCE BEFORE THE NEW SCENE IS RELEASED
Check the actual involved mechanics, state, knowledge, individual speech, scene clarity and production firewall. Fix the material problem locally. Earlier completed research and previous validations are evidence to reuse, not instructions to rerun a whole-project audit after every correction.

CONDENSED SEASONS / NEW-VIEWER CLARITY — COMMITTED
Applies to unwritten Season 2 from XIV.3 and the Season 3 working outline. Retain one primary dramatic conflict, one consequential secondary at most, and at most a necessary seed. A character's participation inside the same conflict is not a demand for a separate subplot. Combine setup, choice and consequence in the same action when causally possible; reorder unwritten beats only within actual travel, treatment, custody, knowledge and choice dependencies. Preserve the existing five S2 dates and fifteen-card S3 working span. Exact prospective incidents remain proposals where labeled.

At a meaningful cut, Black Mimir supplies enough connected orientation to answer: where and when are we now, who matters here and how they relate, what do they want, what stops them, and how does this follow from the previous consequence? Usually weave that into the first action rather than reciting a checklist. Name the actor when pronouns could confuse; make an inside-BELLY/exterior switch and a distant coast/city cut clear. Signpost substantial elapsed days without inventing events in the gap.

A new listener need not know Arcane, RDR2, GOW or previous episodes to understand the immediate decision. Introduce a consequential person through a relevant role, relationship and present action; explain an unfamiliar ability by the result that matters now. Reintroduce a returning relationship briefly when its past changes the choice. Avoid unexplained acronym chains, attendance-list introductions, full biographies and recaps that stall danger. Mimir's memories are his, not information automatically supplied to actors.

Protect the room required for resistance, injury, choices, meaningful banter, reversals and payoff. Cut duplicated exposition and administration; do not compress a consequential fight into a result announcement, omit the cause of a changed relationship or replace the scene with a plot summary. Fast decisive wins are valid; artificial parity, delayed finishers and repeated escapes are not pacing tools. The gang's motives continue to drive the primary conflict. Native choices remain theirs.

Before releasing actual PLAY, do one silent listener pass: can a new listener follow the spatial/action chain and understand why each cut matters without a source file? Repair the local gap in Mimir's connected voice. Then check involved voices, behavior, kit, state, and surviving aftermath together. This is a future drafting requirement, not a claim that WORK has produced or auditioned an episode. The existing 25–30-minute target remains unmeasured guidance, never a reason to pad or force a runtime.

SEASON 3 — 18 APPROVED NARRATION APPROACHES
The ordinary narrator remains first-person Black Mimir. Use these when they help the actual scene; no repeating eighteen-step format. Deadpool's one opening hijack and Mimir's profanely indignant reclamation retain their approved narrow scope.

P01. Open through a present ordinary desire. Let a meal, a place to sleep, a game, a repair or Chris's curiosity establish who these people are before a conflict intrudes. Opening calmly does not require a hidden threat waiting in every paragraph.
P02. A short consequence recap. At the next episode's start, recall only the last result that changes the present choice. When a death matters, name the dead person and actual killer clearly; do not replay the entire season or narrate new reactions as old history.
P03. Establish geography before the remarkable action. Name the dock, room, road or coast and the actors' relative positions before the saber ignites, the serpent moves or a body changes exterior control. Two clear anchors beat a dense mechanism paragraph.
P04. Carry a tangible object between scenes. The lightsaber case, a salvaged part, a meal portion or an actual message can link two actions. Tell the listener who now holds it. Objects do not carry information nobody examined or transport themselves.
P05. Follow the choice after the punchline. A joke can end while the same people continue the task, care or argument. Mimir gives the resulting action and leaves room for emotion. Daniel's humor remains present without erasing sincerity.
P06. Quiet competence deserves narration. Describe one meaningful practical detail rather than a list of skills. Ordinary work need not unlock a power, seed a clue or become a lecture.
P07. Use pairwise scenes to change scale. Move from a public fight to two people handling one consequence. Name who is absent when their presence would otherwise be assumed; don't give everyone an obligatory reaction.
P08. Carry one changed habit forward. A saved portion, waiting for a game save or asking before borrowing an item can show adaptation. Only call it a callback after the originating event has actually happened.
P09. Let Mimir distinguish observation from interpretation. He can suspect vanity, notice coercion or recall a personal history, then admit uncertainty. An actor's claim stays a claim; source knowledge does not broadcast into the gang's ears.
P10. Name the changer, the changed thing and its result in fights. Who struck whom, from where, with what actual effect? End a decisive exchange decisively. Avoid anonymous 'he' chains, unexplained technique names and offscreen death announcements after a vague spectacle.
P11. Make fatalities unmistakable. Show the attacker, victim, fatal action and confirmation plainly. Carry the body, missing person, witness account, damaged access and informed survivors' choices forward as applicable. No grief quota or automatic revenge boss.
P12. Give spectacle a beginning, physical change and aftermath. A major impact displaces real terrain, bodies or water; follow what can now be done or cannot be done. Don't replace the requested strongest event with a silhouette or 'an epic battle happened.'
P13. Use clear elapsed-time bridges. Say that days passed and orient the changed condition; do not claim a twelve-day battle was continuous when it contained actual gaps. Treatment, travel, messages, income and repairs take their real time.
P14. Cut when an action opens another person's consequence. A dispatch can lead to the recipient only after the delivery interval. Thematically related coast/city scenes do not imply adjacency or shared knowledge.
P15. Keep simultaneous strands bounded. Announce the separate place and available cast, then complete one intelligible piece of action before cutting back. The capital, distant coast, Zaun and Matt's ship are not one room.
P16. Introduce newcomers by a present relationship and want. Identify the armed contractor, old family bond or prisoner whose decision matters. Reveal snapshot-relevant depth through conduct, not a source-franchise biography or a roster parade.
P17. Let ordinary life finish. A peaceful scene can close on company, satisfaction, fatigue or silence. Mimir need not predict an ambush, add a clue or tease the next episode. Billy rests consciously; others follow actual biology.
P18. End on an enacted result and chosen direction. Close the consequence rather than adding a larger chamber or a promised rival rematch. If play removes a planned later actor, adjust the outline immediately; a planned function is not immunity.

          "causal_input": "Lee Sin opposes the movement of dangerous seized material through a populated spiritual crossing. The Sins refuse to surrender their entire legitimate haul to a stranger's religious authority.",

      "approved_optional_situations": [
        "All claim founding credit: Dutch calls it his vision, Brian claims the name, Clifford credits his intimidating image; Busta has separate reasons and can dispute Brian.",
        "Introductions become competitive and continue after the audience has begun leaving.",
        "Branding, banner, insignia and entrance consume real resources more enthusiastically than the undertaking; show the cost during action rather than administrative filler.",
        "A modest genuine victory is endangered when they try to stage a more iconic one.",
        "Occasional competent cooperation earns a real opening or prize, followed by an argument over credit; no forced win, humiliation or escape.",
        "They defensively reject questions about what the name means; Dutch can offer an impressive speech that explains nothing.",
        "Their promotion supplies later changing roast callbacks.",
        "Zid's failed insult sometimes gives T.P.P. its best comeback; he may realize too late or think he won. Lumbago is available, not the compulsory end of every failed roast.",
        "Sins dispute who first invented or stole a roast while the opponent is present.",
        "A humiliating nickname competes with T.P.P.'s actual efforts to improve its image; let it arise on page rather than inventing an old incident.",
        "A Sin secretly likes or covets a real piece of their presentation/equipment and is caught inspecting it.",
        "Successful scores provoke family arguments over contributions and the value of ridiculous bonus prizes.",
        "A rehearsed formation/entrance keeps restarting after interruptions; Clifford has removed the helmet, Brusta argue, and Dutch tries to recover his atmosphere.",
        "Busta supplies genuinely good entrance music but Brian's title announcements and Clifford's attempted rhythmic breathing interfere. No new playback device or power is granted.",
        "A private Sins wager over who can irritate Clifford into removing his helmet; methods vary and a useful suggestion can upset the bet. No guaranteed reaction or unsafe removal imposed by comedy.",
        "T.P.P. requests Zid's insults because they are so bad; he can misread this as respect. Family embarrassment changes the exchange.",
        "Billy sincerely covets an enormous T.P.P. ceremonial object, bringing his collecting habit and Chris's shared-space objection into the rivalry. Actual acquisition requires intake/access.",
        "Arthur answers Dutch's imagined tragic confrontation with courteous attention to an embarrassing practical detail, forcing Dutch to answer or evade.",
        "A Sin's roast accidentally supplies a better slogan, T.P.P. adopts it, and the originator resents the enemy using their material.",
        "Both groups pursue the same score for different reasons: contents, public credit, Billy's container, Kaze's destruction. Cooperation can turn into conflict over what counts as winning.",
        "A modest genuine T.P.P. success produces unbearable celebrations and inflated demands. Preserve the real success rather than cancelling it for a gag.",
        "The Sins critique a family member's lazy roast in front of T.P.P.; the rivals discover that internal teasing does not automatically give them the same permission.",
        "Brian attempts a dramatic swivel-chair-style reveal while Busta controls their facing and is addressing somebody else. Actual posture/chair access is required; no separate Brian body.",
        "Busta points out an efficient cruel alternative to a theatrical plan and can act on his own intent; being the practical voice does not make him morally good or automatically victorious.",
        "After Clifford kills the tattoo artist, Dutch gives an extravagant eulogy blaming a cruel world; reminders that their own colleague killed the artist provoke changing rhetoric and a real trust argument.",
        "Optional Dutch tattoo designs: a compass with every direction labeled Tahiti; a crowned mango representing a sovereign people; broken chains that resemble a dog collar. Designs are approved options, not required tattoos or existing marks; every chosen addition needs its session and meaning on page.",
        "Clifford attempts to reclaim TP as Total Punishment; mock expansions change with events, such as Tattoo Pending before repair or Temporary Presence after an actual retreat. Neither a retreat nor the successful rebranding is guaranteed.",
        "During a later tattoo session the others try to keep Clifford from killing the replacement artist, motivated by their need for the artist rather than reform. Preserve the artist's agency and actual consequences.",
        "Brian's pause for fear invites Dutch to start his own speech; their competition for recognition develops through actual responses and choices instead of an endless alternating monologue.",
        "When the Sins dismiss TPP during a major score, TPP may exploit genuine divided attention to take something that matters, then become intolerable about its legitimate success. Optional opportunity, not a forced theft: reach, intent, capabilities and resistance decide; a delivered loss persists."

      "consequence_policy": "Use the existing completed/pending/displaced/no_longer_possible statuses after actual outcomes. Selected recurrence grants no survival.       "scope": "All selected optional repertoire, identities, voices, genre influences and developments retained. Full reset makes old enacted-only-in-S3 outcomes prospective. Latest early formation order controls. Household’s 23 optional situations remain in its unchanged full owner.",
      "character_application": {
        "Brian": "Heavy Dr. Evil energy: theatrical mastermind self-importance, fussy demands for recognition, patronizing deflection, wounded vanity when questioned, and disproportionate revenge presentation. This is a performance/personality influence, not a new identity, headquarters, superweapon, expertise or control power.",
        "Scotty_counterpart": {
          "status": "author_selected_character_dynamic",
          "counterpart": "Busta",
          "application": "Brian treats Busta as a disappointing son when Busta offers a practical objection to his villainous theatrics. Preserve both voices, actual bodily agency and the conjoined body."
        },
        "Dutch_speeches": "Dutch gives the rest of T.P.P. recognizably RDR2-like long speeches about plans, faith, loyalty, freedom and the future, applied to absurd, disproportionate or ridiculous immediate concerns. Preserve his seductive rhetoric, self-justification, manipulation and capacity for genuine feeling. Let the speech itself become a developing comic performance through his changing argument and the listeners' responses/actions; do not reduce this author-selected long-speech behavior to a summary or a mandatory short catchphrase. Length must earn attention, and the current scene still moves.",
        "Dutch_tattoo_progression": "His absurd tattoos accumulate over time. Every new tattoo addition is shown on page in an actual tattoo session, with Dutch explaining the supposedly deep meaning. Preserve any genuinely established marks; do not backfill undocumented tattoos or add new ones between scenes. Real materials, artist access and the small budget matter without long administrative filler.",
        "Clifford_tattoo_trigger": {
          "authority": "Explicit author clarification of existing history, 13 October 2026 Honolulu",
          "established_context": "Arthur and Chris previously stick-and-poked incomplete Clifford tattoos across Clifford's body. Preserve actual established markings; this clarification supplies no additional count, wording variants, placements, dates or unseen episode.",
          "future_application": "The new incomplete TP mark recalls that specific earlier humiliation and is why Clifford is triggered. Let recognition, his reaction and the resulting killing carry the connection; do not substitute generic perfectionism, invent a new flashback scene or replay earlier abuse."
        }
      },
      "villainy_and_interruptions": {
        "evil": "T.P.P. remains a genuinely evil antagonist alliance. Its crimes can injure, kill, exploit or dispossess actual victims and change circumstances. The Sins' contempt is a character response, not proof the villains are harmless or universally laughed at; other people may fear them. Do not turn every attempt into a harmless failed sketch or require a hidden moral justification.",
        "interruption": "T.P.P. can intrude while the Sins are occupied by a genuinely major score or mission, provoking an exasperated come-back-later/fuck-off response. Their reveal can surprise the audience, but they still need a real route, information or credible chance intersection; no teleportation or automatic knowledge of the score. They may choose to exploit the distraction, interfere or demand recognition, and their intrusion can really cost the Sins something.",
        "pacing": "The main objective remains urgent. Rudeness need not dismiss the enemy successfully, speeches do not freeze danger, and a distinct character may keep working while another handles the interruption. Preserve decisive outcomes and the existing no-survival-warranty rule."
      },
      "South_Park_influence": {
